# Concept Index — master register

> **THERE IS NO CONCEPT COUNT IN THIS HEADER ANY MORE. Do not re-add one.**
> Retired 2026-08-09 at the owner's direction. The number is **derived, not
> stored**: run the command below, or read the watcher's row.

**Why a stored count could not be kept honest.** A hand-maintained figure sat at
the top of this file for a month, with a documented command beside it and a
convention that every thread re-measure after adding a row. Threads did. It still
went wrong repeatedly, because **four different commands count this file and they
all return different, individually-correct answers** — the documented row regex,
a looser row regex quoted inside this header's own history, the unique-entry-id
count, and the raw `###` heading count. Measured together on 2026-08-08 they gave
1,792 / 1,799 / 1,792 / 1,800. Twice in four days a careful session took the
number from the wrong line of this header and published it. That is not
carelessness; it is what a stored figure beside three near-identical rivals will
always produce.

Worse, the per-category files had the same disease and nobody was watching it at
all: **all 109 carried a stated count, and 32 of them (29%) were wrong**, summing
to 90 concepts of drift. Those are retired too — same reason, same date.

**Where the count lives now.** `concept-register-drift-check` (DOC-074) reads
this directory daily and writes its verdict to `doc_notes`. It reports the entry
count, the row count, and any concept that has one but not the other:

```sql
SELECT created_at, body FROM doc_notes
 WHERE subject_key = 'concept-register-drift'
 ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1;
```

It reports and never repairs — a missing row needs a summary written by someone
who understands the concept. **It watches the PUSHED branch**, so it cannot see
an entry you have not pushed; run the pair yourself when you edit the register.

**What you still owe when you add a concept:** the entry, and its row in the
table below, **in the same commit**. That has not changed and is the only rule
here that ever mattered. What has changed is that you no longer update a number
at the top, and no longer have three ways to get that number wrong.

```
grep -cE '^\| [A-Z]{2,4}-[0-9]{3} \|' 000_concept_index.md      # rows. THE count command — no number is recorded here on purpose (retired 2026-08-09)
grep -c '^| [A-Z]*-[0-9]' 000_concept_index.md                    # ⚠ THE LOOSE ONE — over-counts, and is NOT the row count. Twice in four days its answer was published as the headline; that is why no number is stored here now
cat *.md | grep -c '^### '                                       # raw headings; always higher — a few are not concept headings

# ── THE DRIFT PAIR. Run BOTH and diff them; the row count alone is blind to
# the failure this pair caught on 2026-08-04 (34 entries with no index row).
cat *.md | grep -oE '^### [A-Z]{2,4}-[0-9]{3}' | sed 's/^### //' | sort -u > /tmp/h.txt   # entry ids
grep -oE '^\| [A-Z]{2,4}-[0-9]{3} ' 000_concept_index.md | tr -d '| ' | sort -u > /tmp/r.txt  # indexed ids
comm -23 /tmp/h.txt /tmp/r.txt   # entries with NO index row — was 34, now EMPTY
comm -13 /tmp/h.txt /tmp/r.txt   # index rows with NO entry — has always been empty
# Why it drifts one way only: adding an entry is two edits in two files and only
# the first is load-bearing for the author, so the index row is the half that
# gets skipped. Nothing before 2026-08-04 ever compared the two.
#
# THIS PAIR NOW RUNS WITHOUT YOU, DAILY — `concept-register-drift-check` (DOC-074),
# a CronJob that reads this directory at a pinned ref and writes its verdict to
# doc_notes. It runs the same four comparisons, including the headline-vs-actual
# one that caught a wrong figure here within seven hours of the last correction.
# Read the latest verdict:
#   SELECT created_at, body FROM doc_notes
#    WHERE subject_key = 'concept-register-drift'
#    ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1;
# It REPORTS and never repairs: a backfilled row needs a summary written by
# someone who understands the concept. Run the commands above yourself when you
# edit the register — the watcher is the safety net, not the procedure.
ls *.md | grep -vc 000_concept_index                             # category files
```

Now the build path counts same-function predecessors among **its own loop's already-rendered items** (loop expansion already publishes `loop_item_index`/`loop_name` on each substep's config and parks items at `datahelpers.LoopItemKey` — declared in `frameworkStepConfigKeys`, already read by `LoopCompleteAction`), which is correct on a page's FIRST build when no rows exist to count; the editor counts stored predecessors position-exactly with a `(position, id)` tie arm matching `loadStoredSections`' tightened ORDER BY. Occurrence 0 survives as the universal fallback and nothing can fail a render. Supersedes the committed plan's DB/slot+rank design, which declared the first-build case its own irreducible residue and needed a config key to activate. ⚠ **the fallback is indistinguishable from success** — a `render_component` step outside the section loop binds 0 silently and a count of *prefixed* ids reads green; count DISTINCT tokens per page. Absent, byte-identical to today, so the roll order is free. THE PIN IS BY ID BECAUSE A NAME DOES NOT IDENTIFY A ROW: `lookupBaseComponent` filters `function` and `forked_from` only — no `component_level`, no `is_active` — then `LIMIT 1` off `is_active DESC, updated_at DESC`, and `[MEASURED 2026-08-25]` **25 of 330** non-forked rows' `function` values carry more than one row, `site-footer`/`site-header` five each spanning `section` AND `site` levels. Same rule as RFC_034 §1. Also cures the advisory's primary path never running the dependent census, so it no longer advises a contract the store is about to divert away from. bugs_open/311's diversion decision is SPLIT and reused verbatim, so a pinned row with foreign dependents still diverts. | IMG-074 | The planner's imagery VOCABULARY (`component_expresses` fifth arm) + `Illustrated Text Block` sources an illustration, not the page hero | **shipped as migration `644` 2026-08-26, `Council-Submitted: 08477888` (read the verdict). DB config, so LIVE ON APPLY — no image, no inert window** | `PLAN_2026-08-25b` §8. ⚠ **A missing WORD, not a missing component** — `component_expresses` fed **three** live planner menus four tokens and none was an image, so `Generic Text Block` and `Illustrated Text Block` read IDENTICALLY and the planner picked the plain one (**208** instances across 23 sites vs **6** on ONE). ⚠ **LANDMINE: `site_assets.image` IS NOT A LITERAL KEY — `imageRoleAliases` maps it to `hero`, unconditionally**, because nothing ever populates `r.assets["image"]`; `[MEASURED 2026-08-26]` every populated value in the estate is a hero asset and **20 of 52** already duplicate an image shown elsewhere on the SAME page. ⚠ **It was a LIVE latent regression**: apis.uk/index resolves that alias, and *live resolution beats `carryStored`*, so its six distinct illustrations were one `plan_sections` run from being overwritten with `hero-home.jpg`. ⚠ **THE TWO HALVES ARE COUPLED** — the obvious exact-match predicate `= 'site_assets.image'` would make the component invisible again after the repoint and the halves would SILENTLY CANCEL; the guard asserts both ends. ⚠ **a changed-row COUNT cannot tell a widening from a reshuffle** (proven by mutation: an arm also suppressing `list` changed the same number of rows) — the assertions are structural, and the count is deliberately NOT asserted because five components were created by other lanes mid-measurement. Schema-derived, not template-derived: **14** components (11 active+section) vs **47** for an `<img` grep. **Residual: supply is the bigger half and is UNTOUCHED** — 26 illustration assets across 5 sites vs 206 heroes across 28, and only **4** `section/illustration` plan rows | imagery.md |

| MDL-044 | Symmetric endpoint-health write — a SUCCESSFUL live call clears the flag its own failures set | **LIVE on chassis v1.0.1334 (2026-08-24), capability-probed BOTH replicas with both controls; **PROVEN on live traffic the same day**: forced false, one successful call cleared it in ~39s, attributed by `last_healthy` landing 61s LATER than `last_checked` — an ordering no other writer can produce (all others set both in one NOW()), with `last_checked` unmoved so no probe ran, `error` cleared to NULL, and a demand control of exactly 1 call. ⚠ Took 5 attempts; 3 proved nothing (2 zero-demand, 1 a harness that could not report success — `psql` renders a bare boolean `t`, a concatenated one `true`) (the estate was quiescent that day: 0 cap failures across 750 successful calls) | `update_endpoint_health` had exactly ONE Go caller and it only ever passed **false**; the sole writer of **true** was the hourly prober. So live traffic could mark an endpoint DOWN and nothing succeeding could mark it UP — and `claim_work_item` gates EVERY work-item claim fleet-wide on that one boolean, so one refused call stopped ALL dispatch until the prober's tick (**measured 60m25s**, 2026-08-17, while 93 of 99 live calls succeeded). ⚠ The prober CANNOT substitute: `pingClaude` returns healthy for any non-auth status and a usage cap is a **400**, so for a cap it is a TIMER that clears the flag, not a health check — an N-consecutive-failures rule belongs on the live-traffic writer, never on the prober, where it would never fire. Flapping under a sustained cap is accepted and is more truthful than a flag pinned false. | model-infrastructure.md |

| WII-024 | The work-item FAILURE-WRITE CONTRACT: one ladder + terminal-decision guard + `retry_after` cooldown + burst-detected transient release, for every Go failure path (`applyWorkItemFailureLadder`) | **LIVE + PROVEN END TO END on v1.0.1322; `bugs_closed/307` CLOSED 2026-08-21** — full canary pass, every arm (307 §9c); councils APPROVED `4cdec68b` r2 + `df0748bf` r1 (the terminal-42P18 repair the acceptance itself found — plus `344`'s completion-vs-cooldown predicate, same build). Outage-scale arm = STANDING WATCH with reopen trigger. Residuals: 344 sweep half, 341/`524_HOLD`, 033 D2 | bugs_open/307 + owner ruling 2026-08-18 ("a transient blip should return the item to queued"). Three defects, one seam: the ladder retried with NO delay (88/100 items burned 3 attempts inside one 2h43m outage); `update_work_item_status` had NO ladder so one failure was terminal in fair weather too (141/270 failed rows in 14d died early, 139 at attempt 1 of 3); neither writer had the terminal-decision guard both COMPLETION-path siblings have. Burst detector keys on normalised `error_message` + >=2 domains AND >=2 agent types — measured over 7 days it fires on 3 signatures, all genuine infra outages, zero single-item faults. Adopts `reaper_policies` numbers (RFC_018's second consumer) | work-item-integrity.md |
| WII-025 | Discovery-check PAGE LIFECYCLE POSTURE registry: a check querying `pages` must declare which axis it takes, mechanically re-checked | **BUILT + COMMITTED 2026-08-22**, test-only so live in CI immediately (the `check_orphan_pages` behaviour half is Go and inert until the next roll); council SUBMITTED; backlog 23 declared gaps | `bugs_open/356`. `pages` has two axes and the predicate family says pair the arms your question needs — but only 3 of 51 pages-querying checks called `PageWantedLivePredicateFor`, the rest hand-spelled it four ways, two of which exclude NOTHING (`COALESCE(status,'') <> 'deleted'`, when only `active`/`archived` exist). 18 checks could route a RETIRED page at a handler; `check_orphan_pages` asked three handlers to make archived pages more reachable and all three already refuse. Four postures (Armed/Observes/Nuanced/KnownGap) with mandatory reasons; SENSOR reads the package dir so it cannot go stale, ASSERTION binds the needle to the declared pages ALIAS, BACKLOG test PRINTS the gap count so debt cannot read as clean. ⚠ NOT "audits should skip archived pages" — `bugs_open/266` warns against exactly that; the discriminator is what the REMEDY does. ⚠ LANDMINE: mutation-proven only on the THIRD attempt — v1 was satisfied by the arm's own COMMENT, v2 by `sni.status='active'` on a joined table (the same false-clean a grep-count gave), and v2's header claimed a limit in the WRONG DIRECTION | work-item-integrity.md |
| WII-026 | `site_work_items.retry_feedback`: a SINGLE-WRITER typed channel carrying a refusal back into the next generation's prompt (`recordRetryFeedback` -> loader -> `call_handler` mapping -> `component-creator` retry block) | **LIVE + DEMAND-PROVEN 2026-08-23** on chassis v1.0.1330; migrations 533/555/561/563/564 all applied; council APPROVED r5 `67b07528`. Built by the `bugfix_311_component_keys` lane under `bugs_open/345`, registered later by the session that picked the bug up | `bugs_open/345`. A rejected component was regenerated from IDENTICAL inputs, so 99 rejections across 3 sites produced ZERO differing retries and one item burned 52 generations. The first fix read `site_work_items.error` — a column with **20 write sites across 10 files** (3 of them the human operator HTTP path): 405 of 799 readable rows were human/lane notes and **35% of what could reach a reader was misattributed**. Remedy is NOT a classifier at the reader but a column with exactly ONE writer, making "is this about my own output?" unanswerable-wrongly; no backfill, deliberately. Demand proof: item `b0ba3e3a` failed at `store_component` 12:09:53Z, its retry carried `last_error`+`last_error_code` and COMPLETED (n=1, no causal claim; pre-fix base rate 3 of 15). ⚠ LANDMINE: `call_handler`'s `input_mapping` is a STRICT allow-list — 533 + the Go loader were live and INERT for a day, and 564 had to repeat 555's wiring for the second key (census: 73 live mappings, zero passed it). ⚠ LANDMINE: the reader is NARROWER than the one it replaced — only failures reaching `:477` can be fed, so the two truncation-shaped checks (returning ~300 lines earlier) write NOTHING; add a failure class upstream and a prompt migration alone renders silence that looks like a fix. ⚠ 345's original success signal (two rejections, different reasons) is UNMET and that is not failure — no post-fix item has been rejected twice | work-item-integrity.md |
| WII-029 | Repeat-failure early termination: an opted-in item whose failure is byte-identical to its recorded one goes terminal early, with a durable `result.terminated_on_repeat` marker (`applyWorkItemFailureLadder`, `stop_on_repeat_failure_item_types`) | **LIVE (mechanism, v1.0.1334+) + APPLIED (opt-in, mig 607, 2026-08-24 20:23Z); marker COMMITTED `7ec5d4f23`, inert until the next roll.** Council APPROVED r1 `f1f1fc37`; completion `Council-Submitted: cf086b8d` | `bugs_open/345` candidate 2. Exact equality NOT `normSigFragment` (it strips quoted strings and would collapse two different invented field names, killing a converging writer); reached only after the transient arm declines (bounded: release cap 10 + burst visibility). ⚠ a firing = "feedback failed" ONLY when the terminal error carries one of the 3 producer validation codes — otherwise it means the class has no feedback channel. ⚠ the MESSAGE side is load-bearing: the mismatch rejection now names every offender deterministically because varying bytes silently opt a class out — the bare `Handler failed` fallback is the standing false-match example (0 rows as of 2026-08-24). ⚠ probe the binary for the `terminated_on_repeat` string literal, NEVER the Go field name, which probes absent while the feature works. | WII-030 | `verify_before_complete`: the registered-verifier gate on `update_work_item_status`'s `complete` arm, opt-in PER STEP — plus a durable `result._verification.status='verifier_not_consulted'` when a step that could have consulted one did not | **LIVE at the artefact 2026-08-25 on `v1.0.1337`** (literal probed on both pods with present+absent controls; the Go identifier is correctly ABSENT). **Inert on every live path by design and still is** (0 steps arm it; none of the **7** reachable item types has a verifier). Four lanes each needed to hold a site's queue, none had a way, so each hand-wrote the same UPDATE and only some left a trace — **six** competing ad-hoc provenance conventions grew in the gap. ⚠ **`p_parked_by` / `p_parked_reason` / `p_release_condition` have NO DEFAULTS** — the stamp is unskippable because it is an argument. `p_apply DEFAULT false`, so a bare call is a dry run. ⚠ **STAMPS `result`, NOT `spec`, and that is not style**: `refreshOpenWorkItemSQL` does `SET summary=$3, spec=$4::jsonb` and REPLACES spec on every re-detection, so a spec stamp dies exactly where a parked row is most exposed; `result` is merged by `v3_site_actions.go:6306` and migration `442` already stamps `result.repair_284`. ⚠ **`unpark` is scoped to ONE `p_parked_by` deliberately — do not widen it**: 60 parked rows carry another lane's LIVE *"un-park after rebuild verify"* condition. Guards are INDUCED (5 refusals called and required to fire) with a positive control (a well-formed dry run must return `applied=false`) and a negative control (the dry run wrote nothing); proven by running the file with `COMMIT`→`ROLLBACK`. **Residual, stated:** nothing stops a session writing a raw UPDATE instead — short of a trigger. Also wires `podmonitor.yaml` into the kustomize build after 26 days live-but-reconciled-by-nothing. ⚠ **does NOT close 040** — the `timeout` residual (146/7d) is undiagnosed | system-architecture.md |
| SYS-093 | An OPT-IN bounded produce retry on the reply lane (`ProduceWithRetry` / `DeliverReply(..., WithRetry(policy))`) + the two transient needles that stop a seconds-long broker blip terminating finished work | **BUILT 2026-08-21, INERT UNTIL THE NEXT CHASSIS ROLL**; behaviour, shipped OPT-IN with the unsafe side OFF (owner 2026-08-02 §2), adopted at FOUR named sites with the five other `DeliverReply` callers byte-identical and pinned; `Council-Submitted:` | `[MEASURED 2026-08-21]` 63 "Kafka write error" + 40 "has no leader" rows across **93 distinct orchestrations** in the retained month — the worst shape a workflow whose every substantive step had committed failing at its terminal `complete_workflow` send, head row FAILED. None of the nine existing needles matched either string, so all of it classified `error_unrecoverable`. kafka-go does not cover it: its writer breaks after ONE attempt on a non-`Temporary` error and `protocol.ErrNoLeader` has no `Temporary()`. ⚠ `"write message to kafka"` DELIBERATELY REFUSED — it wraps every write failure including the deterministic oversize and validation refusals, i.e. the `bugs_open/274` defect; a control test asserts both stay TERMINAL. ⚠ opting in accepts a duplicate reply on a lost ack (no idempotent producer in kafka-go 0.4.47), absorbed by the parent's two-phase `ClaimAwaitedRequest` — so adopt ONLY where the consumer has that dedupe. ⚠ honest negative: the default-OFF pin does NOT discriminate the flag (the zero policy's `Attempts:0→1` clamp covers it in series) — the real mutation is named in the test | system-architecture.md |

Now widening it reaches `/c/`+`/d/` and stops. Enforced not intended: `assertNoDeliveryRoutes` walks the REAL route table at construction and refuses to build the server if a delivery path is mounted on the admin router, so a re-mount crash-loops instead of quietly re-opening the hole. Four guards mutation-proved. ⚠ **NOT a fence-level containment** — 8088 stays reachable from the box for the chat bot's facts relay, so a NEW vhost written straight to :8088 still reaches the admin API; closing that means moving the relay too, deliberately out of scope. ⚠ LANDMINE: `SERVICE_SERVER_DELIVERY_PORT` is set only in the overlay and named in no YAML, and viper's `AutomaticEnv` CANNOT reach a key absent from both file and defaults — without the explicit `BindEnv` the overlay reads perfectly and the listener never starts, 404ing every customer link with no error anywhere. Caught by a test written first, which FAILED before the bind | system-architecture.md |
| DGH-017 | `delivery.Claim` — the owner's pre-delivery review gate + at-most-once handover: the delivery email's precondition layer (may we deliver, have we already, what are the links) | BUILT 2026-08-25, INERT — Go waits on a roll AND on a producer, which does not exist yet; no copy and no send by design; `Council-Submitted: 0b84970d` | The gate is an `approved` needs_delivery_review work item, not a flag or a column, because the owner's 2026-08-21 ruling says the email step must read the item and not something settable by hand; an ABSENT row reads as NO, so forgetting to file the review cannot skip it. The ORDERING is the safety argument: review first, then THE STAMP IS THE CLAIM (idempotent, `AlreadyHandedOver` treated as a refusal), so at-most-once belongs to the stamp and not the send and no retry can send twice — accepting that a stamp may outlive a failed send, which fails LOUD, over send-then-stamp which duplicates SILENTLY. item_key `delivery_review:<site_id>`, one per site; producers: NONE yet, and ⚠ the owed producer MUST go through actions.writeWorkItem or it re-opens the bugs_open/333 bypass closed the same day. ⚠ AdvertisedWindowDays (30) is carried on the result so a composer cannot reach for LiveLinkExpiresAt (42) — the gap is deliberate. ⚠ Absent links are EMPTY, never placeholder URLs that 404 in an inbox. ⚠ Test lesson: the config mutation PASSED first time — sqlmock's refusal of an unqueued call is itself an error, so a bare `err != nil` proved nothing | deployment-github.md |

| PBP-031 | save_page_sections finds its own structured sections (shared default + declared absence + opt-in refusal) | LIVE v1.0.1252 (pod-verified), behaviourally unexercised | Which collected_data path holds a section's content_data was the caller's to remember, so 4 of 6 callers silently wrote NULL — the only thing a re-render can regenerate from; the action now consults the validate gate's own default, reports CONTENT_DATA_REGRESSION, and offers refuse_save_without_sections_metadata as an opt-in refusal (renamed 2026-08-05, code-review F7 — the old spelling collided with a live validate-side key) | page-build-pipeline.md |
| PBP-032 | content_data transport-envelope guard (refuse, or losslessly decode) at the two write seams **and the render seam** | **all three seams LIVE** — storage half proven on v1.0.1252, **render seam LIVE on v1.0.1254 (2026-08-05, both replicas pod-verified with controls); `bugs_closed/199`** | The LLM text-path envelope {type:text, result:<string>} was stored verbatim as content_data — the source every reasoned rerender regenerates from — so a good-looking page carried an armed regression; the guard decodes only on the parser's two lossless provenance tiers and REFUSES on every lossy one, keyed on the envelope signature rather than the key count. **The render seam (2026-08-05) applies the same normaliser one step earlier**, because the write guards cannot speak for what a component RENDERS and the render gate is blind to 315 of 1212 live page_components rows (components declaring no required source:'llm' field) | page-build-pipeline.md |
| PBP-033 | duplicate-section collapse at save_page_sections (the save's first self-comparison) | built, inert till roll | Seven guards on this save all compared the incoming set against EXISTING rows or a floor and none compared it against ITSELF, so a doubled list passed all of them and vonc served a whole page twice; collapses only entries indistinguishable in every column the INSERT binds, honours plan-specified repetition, records the adjacency signature the producer hunt lacked | page-build-pipeline.md |
| PBP-034 | page_components UNIQUE invariant on byte-identical sections (DB-level, agrees exactly with PBP-033) | applied, enforcing | PBP-033's Go guard covers 1 of 7 writers and "the other six write single rows" is a fact about current callers, not an enforced mechanism; this makes it structural — and the two intuitive index shapes are wrong, one of them refused by Postgres itself against a legitimate live pair | page-build-pipeline.md |

| PLAN-048 | Twin-identity reconciliation: layered matching in the reconciler + a writer guard that stops the identity being re-derived | LIVE + BEHAVIOURALLY PROVEN (v1.0.1321 as at 2026-08-21): a replan that minted 19 phantoms pre-fix minted 0 post-fix on the same site/config. bugs_open/215 CLOSED -> bugs_closed/215; residual damage in bugs_open/347 (4 live pairs, owner decision) and bugs_open/340. honour_realised_identity seeded on 3 sites; the three twin switches still default OFF | Prevents a re-plan minting a second pages row for a page already realised under another spelling; 71 live rows are not fixed points of CanonicalisePage, so the reconciler alone cannot hold — both write surfaces must honour the realised identity. ⚠ Until 08-19 the flag was UNREACHABLE for a page the planner named exactly as stored (19 phantoms on a live canary, and the dark-launch counters could not see the shape) — Pass B2 now stamps it. Deliberately refuses both-deployed pairs (owner decision) and same-name pairs whose roles disagree | site-plan-and-reconciler.md |
| PLAN-049 | `plan_includes_tools` — the planner's component menu includes the site's OWN tool components (per-site opt-in) | LIVE 2026-08-14 (migration 407, config-only); enabled on loancalculator.co.uk only; md5-proven inert for every unflagged site | Two gates in series: structure-spec opt-in AND tool already placed on the planning site's own pages (the library is global — 81 tools). Fifth opt-in key on the structure aspect. Param `site_record.site_id` from ensure_site in the same linear workflow — the binding write_site_plan has always used. Pairs with the matchLockedRow identity arm so a rebuild keeps calculators in place | site-plan-and-reconciler.md |
| PLAN-050 | `menu_field` — validate_site_plan ACCEPTS the component menu its own planner step was shown | built 2026-08-16, config LIVE (migration 439), Go INERT until the next chassis roll (`5534e9f71`) | The ACCEPT half of a menu widening. An opt-in step-config key names the collected-data path holding the menu (`available_components`); those rows are UNIONed into componentNameResolver, so a widened OFFER (PLAN-049) can no longer be silently deleted by an un-widened acceptance whitelist (PLAN-027, `bugs_open/282`: 12 tool sections proposed, 12 dropped). Single-sources the predicate instead of copying it across the SQL/Go line. Absent key = today's behaviour; the shared resolver's query is deliberately unchanged so apply_gap_plan is unaffected | site-plan-and-reconciler.md |
| PLAN-051 | `datahelpers.PageSlotRow` — ONE reader for a page's STORED slot identity (`page_components.slot_name` → component), shared by every surface that maps a section name to a component | **LIVE 2026-08-21 at v1.0.1322**, binary-probed with controls; 83 of 88 unresolvable names covered (commit A of the `bugs_open/204` lane) | The same blindness had been fixed three times in three private loaders (182 re-render, 13252f714 build, and the validation path that DELETES rather than defers); a fourth copy is how the third came to exist. One shared predicate, two projections, two PURE derivations — `SlotIDMap` (conflict rule: disagreeing repeats dropped) and `SlotNameSet` (membership, which deliberately does NOT inherit it, because answering "no" on a legitimate repeat deletes a live section). Per-page scoping means it can preserve a name the page already carries but can never authorise a new placement — so it is NOT the resolver widening PLAN-050's landmine forbids | site-plan-and-reconciler.md |
| PLAN-052 | `pages.sections` is non-destructible by a sync — an EMPTY incoming list cannot overwrite a real one, and `recompose_pages` is the one door | **LIVE 2026-08-21 at v1.0.1322**, all four directions proven against real Postgres in a rolled-back transaction (commit C of the `bugs_open/204` lane) | `sections = EXCLUDED.sections` was unconditional while its nav_label/meta_description siblings in the SAME statement got destructive-write guards on 08-19; on 08-20 that clause turned one planner defect into 41 emptied live pages. The plan stays authoritative for every NON-empty proposal — only the non-empty->empty transition is intercepted, and deliberate emptying travels through the existing recompose_pages release, so no operator has to remember a new flag. Refusals are durable, not logged. LANDMINE: two of five mutations SURVIVED first pass — sqlmock never executes SQL, and a guard tested at the helper's boundary is untested against the wiring that decides its input | site-plan-and-reconciler.md |
| PLAN-053 | `component_expresses()` — what a component can EXPRESS, derived from its own template and schema, printed in every planner menu | **LIVE 2026-08-24** — 591-593 applied+recorded 16:53Z, council `ca400ba6` APPROVED; every guard mutation-proven to refuse with passing controls, and the evidence gate proven to discriminate in BOTH directions | Returns `html-block`/`list`/`table`/`items`/`{}` from the template's literal markup and the schema's field types, called at READ time by the three planner menus so nothing is stored, backfilled or able to drift while the RFC_032 lane rewrites html_template fleet-wide. Replaces `content_shape` in intent — that column has ZERO Go readers, is absent from the birth INSERT, is NULL on 128/151, and is WRONG on 12 rows claiming structured_list with no list markup. ⚠ SELECT and listing line must ship in ONE migration (`missingkey=zero` prints `<no value>`). ⚠ NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT: the library has no generic checklist/steps/table/calendar component, so this informs a choice it cannot supply | site-plan-and-reconciler.md |

In scope = platform code OR a migration `NNN_name.sql` **including `_HOLD`** (it is the change, held for ordering, hand-applied — excluding it was a real defect the council caught); only `_ROLLBACK`/`_VERIFY`/`_SUPERSEDED` and prose are refused. ⚠ the three consumers need OPPOSITE failure semantics (097 loud/exit 1 not 2, nudge silent/exit 0, 098 loud) — do not unify them. ⚠ tooling stays out, so the gate's own repairs still need an explained `FORCE=1` | fix-loop.md |

| SQLC-002 | `platform/livespec` — the declaration of a live DB object, in a file ALLOWED to change | **BOTH PHASES LIVE** — phase 1 2026-08-22 (4 guards, council `b3676918` APPROVED r2, `873575ecf`); **phase 2 DEPLOYED 2026-08-23** (CronJob `live-declaration-drift-check`, `0 7 * * *`, verified at the artefact 08-25) | A migration is append-only history (`schema_migrations` checksums the FILE), so a guard reading one is pinned to a frozen snapshot BY CONSTRUCTION — `TestDivergenceVocabularyMatchesMigration357` failed with "migration 357 no longer contains X", an event the checksum rule already made impossible, and two more `t.Skipf`'d to green on a rename. livespec is the declaration in a mutable file; guards assert against it. ⚠ Keyed on the OBJECT not the migration (506 writes TWO live objects). ⚠ Auditor-only entries are COUNTED (`LiveAuditOnlyDeclarations`, renamed from `DeferredDeclarations` 2026-08-25) so a declaration no Go test reads cannot read as guarded — **8** as of 2026-08-26, of **12** declarations. ⚠ **`FragmentMatch` is presence-only, so it is BLIND to a live object GAINING a value, and a `Max` bound does NOT fix that** — only a paired `CountEqual` does. Closed 2026-08-26 for both `constraint.*` entries and page-content-writer, enforced by `TestEveryFragmentMatchDeclarationIsGainVisibleOrWaived`; still waived for the 2 `trigger_fn` bodies. ⚠ **The live object misdescribes ITSELF** — the live `claimed-item-timeout` `pre_query` still states the superseded "LOCKSTEP TWIN of the RegisterVerifier() calls" contract and names a DELETED test, and that sentence is the original cause of `bugs_closed/317`. Phase 2 (the daily live-vs-declaration auditor) is LIVE and clean 3 runs running; `bugs_open/363` stays OPEN for its residuals — the gain-blindness above, that self-misdescribing `pre_query`, and `bugs_open/375`'s queued registration — not for the deploy | sql-change-management.md |

LANDMINES: timestamps CANNOT answer this (candidate 4's `last-modified` method returned 40 of 40 false positives on healthy pages, persisting 85 minutes); it has NO live positive (228 of 228 pages match today) so it is a regression guard proved by induced faults, not by production; the 30-minute settle window is load-bearing (3 measured divergences at ages 1–14s were all healthy in-flight deliveries); and it depends on every `deployed` stamper being armed — **6 of 6 now are** as of 2026-08-23 (~~3 of 6 are NOT~~; the first census walked one level and missed the three nested in `config.sub_workflow`; migration 526 REFUSES to enable the check while any is unarmed). **⚠ ITS ONLY PRODUCTION FINDING WAS A FALSE POSITIVE — withdrawn 2026-08-23**: a Cloudflare Web Analytics beacon injected at the edge, not a stale page, so on such a zone the check can never match and re-flags every pass. Config half live (migration 542 opts css-patch-agent's `deploy_css` in at 0.5) | `git_commit` had no size guard of any kind, so a commit replacing a 17-26KB site stylesheet with a few hundred bytes of patch rules succeeded silently — nine sites across three waves (`bugs_open/198`). | VIZ-016 | Render-audit selector contract: composed IN the page and VERIFIED there | built, INERT until both images roll | A filed selector is asserted to select the very element measured (`indexOf.call(querySelectorAll(sel), el)`), carries `matches`, and declares `summary.selector_scheme`. | VIZ-018 | Cascade attribution: the audit records WHICH declaration wins, and PROVES it by removing it | ~~built 2026-08-25, INERT until both images roll~~ LIVE + EXERCISED 2026-08-26 (first audit attributed + 5 repairs landed; inherited-colour class is unattributable by design; retraction proof open to 08-29) | The probe finds the declaration deciding an element's `color` and proves it: removing a LOSING declaration cannot change the computed value, so if the value moves the removed one WAS the winner — sound whatever heuristic picked it, and it under-claims rather than guesses (`verified:false` routes as `unattributed`). Records surface/selector/!important/document-order; the chassis computes `min_specificity` with cascadia and writes `repair_surface` + `override_requirement`. Fixes aiming every contrast repair at a theme that cannot govern the pixel: [MEASURED 2026-08-25] 33 of 40 sampled completions were out-specified by a page-level rule, 0 lived in the theme. ⚠ foreground only; ⚠ item_key deliberately unchanged | visualisation-and-charts.md |
| VIZ-017 | The three GENERIC structured components: checklist, period-calendar, comparison-table | **LIVE 2026-08-24** — 604/605/606 applied+recorded, council `c134b0e9` APPROVED; all three appear in the live build-site-planner menu for the site that motivated the bug. Live but UNEXERCISED — no page built with them yet | The vocabulary the library never had, and the other half of PLAN-053 — which was recorded as NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT because all 44 structural components were special-purpose. Real `<ul>` / `<ol>` / `<table>`; no numeric, price, rating or score field in any of them (asserted by regex over the schema); no `<svg>`; `{{.InstanceID}}` from birth. ⚠ REUSE REMOVED A FOURTH: no `steps` component, because mechanism-flow (VIZ-006) already draws an ordered process. ⚠ period-calendar is NOT the editorial lane's fact-fed timeline — a recurring cycle, no dates, no citations; a year in a period label means you want theirs. ⚠ comparison-table's claims risk is STATED not solved: no figure-shaped field exists, but cells are free text and 29 of 48 sites have no evidence base | visualisation-and-charts.md |
| VIZ-019 | The render audit's COVERAGE CURSOR: a capped sweep rotates, and the pages awaiting a grade ride every run | **LIVE AND ACCEPTED 2026-09-03** — cursor on `v1.0.1354` + migration 660; reader as CronJob `render-truncation-check` 07:50 UTC, fired on its own schedule 09-03. Acceptance: union **151 of 151 distinct live pages, ZERO missed**, over 3 scheduled runs. Councils `f67593f5` + `f49da30d` APPROVED. `bugs_closed/394` | `request_render_audit` took the FIRST `max_pages` of `ORDER BY COALESCE(nav_order,999), name` on EVERY run, so pages past the cap had never been audited and never would be. [MEASURED 2026-08-26] webdesign.co.uk 146 live / 60 audited / **86 never**, and the tail was a CLASS — all 45 `tool-*-guide` at nav_order 200, unreachable at ANY cap below 98 on a site that grew 15 pages in two days, so `bugs_closed/242`'s 25→60 mitigation was outgrown. Opt-in keyset cursor (`rotate_coverage`, default FALSE) in that same ordering, per `(site_id, agent_type)` in new table `render_audit_page_cursor`, advanced at DISPATCH and written AFTER the produce. ⚠ THE WINDOW IS A UNION, because a plain cursor takes per-page grading latency to ~9 days and migration 469 is an OWNER INSTRUCTION that seven was unacceptable — the pages with an open `contrast_failure` (3 of 146; bounded at max_pages/2 so coverage cannot stall) ride EVERY run. Preserved: grading latency. Traded: head-page DETECTION 3d→one cycle, in exchange for the tail going from never to one cycle — state both. ⚠ NEVER PARSE a `contrast_failure` item_key: a selector may contain `#` AND a page URL may (idea.uk has `/tools.html` and `/tools.html#audience-check` both active) — match forward with `workItemKey(...)`+HasPrefix, the grader's own construction. Council caught this. | visualisation-and-charts.md |

| CLM-031 | A malformed fact costs THAT FACT: EvidenceBase.MalformedFacts + the malformed_evidence_fact item | built+council-APPROVED c2d1d570+committed 3f221f99f/e5b41dc31, INERT till the chassis roll | Decoding `facts` as ONE array meant a single undecodable fact returned an error for the WHOLE base, and all three claims gates read that as "this site has no register" — so the site's banned_claims went with it. MEASURED 2026-09-03 over all 27 live registers: 2 were dead this way, 10 bans inert, one since 08-24, found by audit and never by any signal. CTE-only scheduled task, one producer, no automated consumer. Report is THREE NUMBERS on every tick (missing_total/filed_new/already_open) so a missing log line means it did not run and `missing_total=0` is positive health — deliberately not the shorter no-rows form, which collapses healthy with already-queued. `needs_human_review` + empty handler because the posture rung is a Q4 RECORD a person signs, not something a sweep infers. ⚠ the anti-slop benefit is BOUNDED: ScanUnregisteredNumbers does not arm on guide/blog-post/tool bodies, and that exclusion is measured. | WII-013 | Verifier remit contract (`VerifierPolicy.Grades`) + the producer sets for `hardcoded_section_colors` / `dark_section_audit` | BUILT + COMMITTED 2026-08-10 (`2d151c41f`, `bugs_open/213`); Go INERT until the next roll — NOT in v1.0.1283. Council SUBMITTED, verdict unread (corr `c9c7c83f`) | `item_type` is the join between WHO FILED a work item and WHAT PREDICATE regrades it at completion, and the registry treats the NAME as proof the two match. A second producer filing under a verified type gets graded by the first producer's predicate — which answers ITS OWN question correctly, returns `Resolved:true`, and closes the item `complete` untouched. Nothing errors; the specification is the defect. MEASURED: 11 of 11 design-audit items complete, NONE ever failed to close, against 8 failures from the producer who wrote the verifier; the worked case closed in 3m17s against a component last touched 10.5h BEFORE the item existed. Fix in two halves: `dark_section` gets its own item_type, and `VerifierPolicy.Grades` lets a verifier disclaim an item it does not speak for (routes into WII-011's fail-closed machinery as `verifier_scope_mismatch`). It keys on the ROW (`target.Spec`), NEVER on a producer list — that design is refuted, since any agent definition can file any item_type from DB config with no code change, and `created_by` bottoms out at the literal `generic`. Opt-in, unsafe default OFF (2026-08-02 shared-seam ruling). LANDMINE: `spec->>'audit_source'` is the ONLY thing naming a producer — a census grouped by `item_type` hides the split. And a `complete` count is NOT a false-complete count: of the 11, one is confirmed false at the artefact and one measures clean | work-item-integrity.md |
| WII-014 | Third `capability_gap` kind: `GapRuleMissing` — "the DETECTOR has no rule here, so it examined nothing" | **LIVE + FIRED AT THE ARTEFACT 2026-08-12** (v1.0.1291, probed per replica; first row on vetcomparison.uk 17:15Z) | `remit.go`'s two gap kinds both describe a HANDLER (narrow remit / not registered). A check branching on a site's own recorded shape returns an EMPTY result for a shape it has no branch for, and empty is byte-identical downstream to "examined and clean": the silence reads as a clean bill. `GapRuleMissing` files ONE undispatchable `capability_gap` saying the check examined nothing. **The switch IS the declaration — no Go-side list of known shapes**, because the authority is the live `domain-strategist` prompt and a mirror is permanently one config edit behind; a shape is examined by HAVING A CASE, everything else files, so a seventh revenue model or a mis-cased row arrives as a ROW rather than as silence. To make a shape silent on purpose, give it a case with the reason — that is a refusal a reviewer can see; falling through is what an omission looks like. `capabilityGapSummary`'s own `default:` arm was committing the same defect and now names the kind it did not recognise | work-item-integrity.md |
| WII-015 | `verifier-remit-check`: the daily CLASS detector for the verifier/producer join (`VerifierDeclaresRemit` + item_type `verifier_remit_gap`) | BUILT + COMMITTED 2026-08-11 (`bugs_open/213` owner ruling D3); CronJob image on its OWN tag sequence, so it is live when its overlay is applied, not when the chassis rolls; council r1 REVISE → r2 submitted, corr `fc082c4a` | WII-013 gave a verifier the ability to DISCLAIM an item it does not grade — but `Grades` is OPT-IN, so the NEXT producer converging on any other verified item_type reproduces `bugs_open/213` unless a human remembers. Producers are clustered from the ROW (audit_source split, then key-sets joined at overlap ≥0.5): a raw key-set count fires on FOUR single-producer types, and Jaccard invents a second producer for `page_canonical_collision` (11 keys vs 3 sharing 2 → J=0.167, overlap 0.667). `created_by`, the `source` column and the `spec.check` VALUE were each measured and rejected — all three fire on types with one producer. N=3 is arithmetic: 7-of-7 re-reports bounds the per-run miss rate at `1-0.05^(1/7)`≈35%, so one silence closes a live defect ~35% of the time, three ~4.2% — the first N under 5%. | WII-037 | `refuse_untraceable_park()`: the database refuses a park that does not say who made it | ✅ **LIVE AND PROVEN AT THE ARTEFACT 2026-09-02 16:16Z** (`a027bf03b`, migration `690`) — applied by hand and recorded; trigger attached and `tgenabled='O'`; self-test 6 assertions before COMMIT, then the independent `_VERIFY` passed all 6 against the live trigger (exit 0, ROLLBACK, zero litter). Council **APPROVED** `dcd2b3c9` (4 advisory, none high) — ⚠ **and the approving round found a REAL hole: `690` exempts every update to an already-deferred row, so a shelf row can be RE-POINTED to a named handler without touching status, reaching the forbidden shape. Induced live: ACCEPTED. `690`'s own VERIFY asserted that write as CORRECT. CLOSED by migration `700` (`1f0cd8ae2`), APPLIED AND PROVEN 2026-09-02 — the same probe that said HOLE CONFIRMED now says HOLE CLOSED, and the corrected VERIFY passes all 7 (it failed at 5b before 700). Guard now complete on BOTH entry paths** | `bugs_open/396`'s standing residual. `park_work_items()` required who/why as arguments but nothing stopped a raw `UPDATE ... SET status='deferred'`; such a row with a NAMED handler is selected by nothing and still holds its `idx_swi_dedup` slot, so the detector cannot re-file and another session hits 23505 — one blocked `bugs_open/328` for 22 days. Refuses the TRANSITION into `deferred` with a named handler unless the write carries `parked_by` AND `parked_reason`, in `spec` OR `result` (389 uses one, the verb the other). ⚠ Scope set by census not by the bug file: `deferred` + EMPTY handler is the legitimate `bugs_closed/077` shelf — 2,656 rows, 0 provenance, five producers — and a guard requiring provenance on every `deferred` write would have refused all of them. | DOC-077 | `report-register-version-lag` — how far behind the fleet is each entry's cited chassis version, keyed on the register's FIELDS not its prose | built + runnable 2026-08-12, read-only, cluster-optional, deliberately NOT scheduled and NOT a checker | Reports "this entry's evidence has expired", never "this entry is wrong". 273 citations / 156 entries; keyed to `status:`+`status-evidence:` (current-state claims by the register's own convention) 206 / 139, of which 111 are 50+ behind. **The negative result is worth more than the tool: classifying citations by their prose left 77% unclassified**, because "deployed in v1.0.1029" (permanent fact) and "both replicas of v1.0.1218 return X" (expiring verification) are indistinguishable by pattern. One sharp class — a container image tag quoted directly — because all 187 live `agent_definitions` rows carry the live tag, so a quoted tag dates the observation. Corrected `SYS-077` (883 behind, row is on v1.0.1290) while `HITL-020`'s identical v1.0.407 is NOT stale (it describes a seed file). LANDMINE: use an adjacency test not a proximity window (2/7 → 9/9 precision), and print the CITATION's context not the line head — three correct hits read as false positives from a truncated line | documentation-system.md |

| RSH-008 | `agenterrors`: the ONE `agent_error_log` writer, below the import cycle that forced 19 copies | deployed (LIVE v1.0.1262) — **provenance hardening LIVE on v1.0.1268, pod-proven both replicas 2026-08-08** | Writer moved to a LEAF package both sides of `orchestration -> actions` can import; 19 hand-copied INSERTs (8/9/10/11/13 cols, `orchestration_id` absent from nine) converted onto it. | RSH-009 | `ExecutionContext.ResolvedAgentType` — one ladder for "which agent is running", shared by the two packages that each answered it | built (inert until the next chassis roll; NOT pod-proven) | `RunAgentType` then `Sender.AgentType`, hoisted onto the shared type so `coordinator.determineOwnerAgentType` and `actions.runningStepProvenance` stop disagreeing — a row filed under "the running step" recorded the dispatch sender (`generic`) while the orchestration row for the same run recorded the real agent. The pod's `AGENT_TYPE` and the `generic` filler deliberately do NOT move: each consumer's tail differs, and a `fallback` parameter would be the same drift in a shared-helper costume. ⚠ the commissioning handoff's sizing was STALE — 499 of the 555 `generic` rows predate `RunAgentType` itself, so the case is structural, not volume. | resilience-self-heal.md |
| RSH-010 | `retryWindow` — a replayed request waits the window its STEP DECLARED | **LIVE on v1.0.1309** both replicas (build point `f0117fb8b`, controls both ways) and **BEHAVIOURALLY PROVEN 2026-08-18 18:28Z** — `call_dispatch` rv1 granted **00:15:00**, its declared 900s, `processed` inside it, against **219 pre-roll retries none above 05:00**; council **APPROVED round 3**, corr `7c92389a` | The block it replaced derived the retry window from the awaited ROW and then capped it — >30min declarations fell to **3 minutes**, everything else to **5** — so the longer a step declared the LESS it got, an INVERSION across **33 steps / 25 agent types** (up to an 86400s human-approval step given 3 min on retry). The row was a poisoned source too: `UpdateAwaitedRequestRetry` resets `sent_at`, so from retry 2 it reports the previous RETRY's window. Measured: `call_dispatch` 15:00 at rv0 → 05:00 at rv1-3; `build-dispatch-loop` exceeds 5 min in 25.5% of COMPLETED runs but its declared 15 min in only 5.9%. ⚠ **029 part A ONLY — does NOT close it**; what kills a child's continuation post-handshake is OPEN. ⚠ a DORMANT TWIN (hard 30s) survives in `helpers.go retryTimedOutRequest`, declared not fixed | resilience-self-heal.md |
| RSH-011 | `wedge-evidence-capture` — 029's evidence preserved before retention destroys it | **LIVE 2026-08-19**, hourly CronJob at `:17`; capture path **PROVEN BY INDUCTION** (5 real orchestrations captured with their full `awaited_requests` sequence, re-run captured 0 = dedupe holds, induced rows deleted) | 029's wedge fired **18 times in 4 hours on 08-17** and **every row was gone by 08-19**. The reaper terminates a frozen row at **4h** and live `database-cleanup` **DELETES** `EXECUTING_STEP` at **4h** — the same threshold — so reaped-only evidence is a **lower bound**; this captures LIVE wedges at freeze+30min instead. ⚠ the repo seed says 7d/24h, the LIVE `pre_query` says **24h/4h**. ⚠ `doc_notes.subject_type` is CHECK-constrained to 8 values — `orchestration` is not one | resilience-self-heal.md |
| RSH-012 | The awaited park is keyed on request IDENTITY and its outcome is TYPED (`awaitedResponseIDMarker` + `parkOutcome` + the table cross-check at the advance decision) | **BUILT 2026-08-21, INERT UNTIL THE NEXT CHASSIS ROLL**; unit- and MUTATION-proven (restore presence-keying and the wedge test fails with the park skipping and issuing no UPDATE); `Council-Submitted:`, no verdict read yet | `343` (silent post-abandonment freeze; lane `docs024_key_docs_latest/bugfix_029_retry_kills_live_child/`): the arrival check asked only "is there a `response` key under this step name?" — true of EVERY iteration after the first on a loop step — so each re-registration of an answered step name read as an arrival, the park returned bare `nil` WITHOUT persisting, and the caller read `nil` as success and inserted an orphan `awaited_requests` row + armed a timeout for a request no map knows about. Records WHICH request a reply answered, in ALL FOUR response branches (two wrote no marker at all; `output_mapping` is LIVE on real `call_agent` paths). `parkOutcome` makes "success without persisting" unrepresentable. Advance decision now cross-checks the table: detection UNCONDITIONAL (`error_code=await_divergence`), enforcement opt-in via WFA-021. ⚠ does NOT close 343 — the FIRST death is unexplained. ⚠ the table is a cross-check, never a naive replacement for the map's optimistic-lock CAS | resilience-self-heal.md |
| RSH-013 | The loop-skip advance persists with the same optimistic-lock retry discipline as every other advance, and the awaited row goes terminal only once it is durable | **BUILT 2026-08-21, INERT UNTIL THE NEXT CHASSIS ROLL**; mutation-proven with each mutation's kill set RECORDED, not assumed (M1 unretried write, M2 mark-before-persist, M3 mark-on-error-path); `343` P2 — **a PATH repair: verified as a path, UNVERIFIED as ever having fired, does NOT close 343** | `skipToNextLoopIteration` wrote its advance with ONE unretried `repo.UpdateState`. An optimistic-lock failure there lost the advance — and on the async path the awaited-map delete with it, while `MarkAwaitedRequestComplete` had ALREADY run, so the row was terminal and the reply's redelivery was eaten by the `processed_at` duplicate guard: a lost continuation, on exactly the path carrying all 31 of 343's terminal outcomes. Every sibling advance already retries (park 10, handleCompleteResponse 15, progress 5); the asymmetry IS the defect. ⚠ do NOT substitute `UpdateStateWithRetry` — it reloads and does `*state = *reloaded`, discarding the caller's mutations and persisting a no-op | resilience-self-heal.md |
| RSH-014 | `migration-ledger-drift-check` — the free, mandated, already-built "applied by hand and never recorded" migration check, now driven on a daily clock | **DEPLOYED AND LIVE 2026-09-02**, proven by two manual triggers against production (second one clean: single pod, exit 0, real `doc_notes` row read back — 158 pending/81 clean/72 flagged) | `run-migrations.sh`'s own dry run already computes this for free and CLAUDE.md already mandated running it; nobody drove it on a schedule, so `bugs_open/426` found 7 unnoticed-for-a-month instances, and a live re-check the day this shipped found 34. Fetches real migration files via a sparse/shallow/partial `git clone` (1.8s for 16MB, no repo history) and runs the runner UNMODIFIED (only `PSQL_CMD`/`MIGRATIONS_DIR` overridden) — zero duplicate vocabulary. Buckets ALREADY+DUP+INCONCLUSIVE together as NEEDS REVIEW, not just ALREADY (a hand-applied-unrecorded file's guard can word its message differently). ⚠ copied `single-owner-carriers-check`'s "exit 1 on findings" convention at first, then had to CORRECT it after the first live run — that check's findings are rare, this one's are the daily norm, so exiting 1 caused a full re-run and a permanently `Failed` Job; fixed to exit 0 on any successful report. Candidate 2 (probe's prose-matched `ALREADY` verdict) deliberately NOT fixed — separate, higher-stakes change, deferred. | resilience-self-heal.md |
| WFA-011 | Shared-`output_field` check — CLI **and** the standing `shared-output-fields-check` CronJob | deployed (CLI + CronJob LIVE, daily 07:10 UTC, first run proven clean 2026-08-08) | Flags two steps sharing an `output_field` that are transitively reachable over the FULL routing graph and are different actions. Ack-file ratchet reports NEW pairs and STALE acks, and travels IN the image. Reports to `doc_notes` on EVERY run incl. clean, so a missing row means the job did not run. LANDMINE: a hand-written walk into `sub_workflow` cannot see `substeps`, the half the executor PREFERS — use `validation.WalkSteps`. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-012 | A workflow config dot-path may index an array (`search_results.results.0.url`) | built, council-APPROVED — roll state NOT grep-verifiable, see entry | `datahelpers.ExtractNestedField` indexes a `[]interface{}` when a segment is a bare in-range non-negative integer; map access is tried FIRST and is unchanged, so a literal "0" key still wins. The change is control flow with no new string literal, so no pod-grep marker exists (DOC-073's case) — its lane owns the live proof | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-013 | Optional-key BUDGET census (`config-key-audit --optional-key-budget`, `scripts/audit-optional-key-budget.sh`) | built 2026-08-13; **budget RULED 2026-08-14: N=10** (wrapper default); cron half still the owner's call | RFC_022's counter: `len(spec.Optional)` per action joined against DISTINCT live carriers (WalkSteps); `over_budget` fires only on SHARED (≥2 carriers) actions past N=10. Standing stock: analyse_repo_local 12, append_doc_note 11 (8 carriers), diagnose_prepare_fix_commit 11 — each owes ONE review of its accumulated surface, then baselined. Owner framing: sharing is estate DESIGN, never the defect. Wrapper reads refusal from EMPTY stdout, never exit code (`go run` folds exit 2 into 1) | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-014 | The awaited-park carries the dispatching step's own `collected_data`, additively (`carryCollectedDataOntoFreshState`) | built + tested 2026-08-15, **NOT live** (Go — inert until the next chassis roll) | RFC_012 (a), owner ruling 2026-08-15. Parking a step to await a reply used to discard every in-memory `CollectedData` mutation (`persistAwaitingStateWithRetry` copied only AwaitedRequests/Status/LastActivity onto a fresh DB load), so an action's own keys never reached the DB and the reply-time merge "preserved" a map that never held them. Now carried ADDITIVELY — a key already on the fresh copy is never overwritten, so an arrived reply still wins. Guard: a carried `response` key under an awaited step would forge the arrival signal, so it is stripped by copy. LANDMINE: this fixes the PARK only — `applyResponseToState` still REPLACES wholesale on its `output_mapping` and default branches, so a carried key can still die at reply time; a direct DB write before dispatch is still the only thing surviving both. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-015 | `loop_iteration_terminal`: a reserved step-config key telling a loop's per-iteration terminal NOT to aggregate | built + tested 2026-08-17, **NOT live** (Go — inert until the next chassis roll); `Council-Submitted: 7a3c4fb7` | `bugs_open/289`. A loop sub-workflow's terminal substep is declared `action: loop_complete` — the same action as the loop's OWN end-step — and expansion injects it once PER ITERATION, so the whole-loop aggregator ran inside every lap and its generic scan copied every `<loop>_iter_<i>_*` key, earlier aggregates included: `collected_data` DOUBLED per lap (measured exact 2.00x; `tool-auditor` hit 22 MB and completed 1 run in 63; `build-dispatch-loop`, the fleet dispatcher, reached 13 MB). Expansion now marks the injected copy and `LoopCompleteAction` returns a 3-field marker for it; aggregation stays on the `<loop>_complete` end-step, which is what `output_field` exposes. Blast radius MEASURED as empty: all 15 such substeps declare no `output_field` and no `next_step`, so the value was addressable by nothing. LANDMINE: match on the substep's ACTION, never its name — it is called `done` in only 9 of 15 loops (`complete_page` x4, `complete_dispatch`, `task_complete`), so a name-based fix leaves 6 doubling. Residuals left open: the `total_iterations` fallback, and no size tripwire on `collected_data`. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-016 | `collected_data` size tripwire — the fleet can SEE a run whose state is running away | built + tested 2026-08-17, **NOT live** (Go — inert until the next chassis roll); **in council round 2 of corr `7a3c4fb7` (16:38Z)** — ~~no council trailer, the gate is unreachable until the Anthropic quota returns 2026-09-01~~ CORRECTED 2026-08-17: that outage was ~3 MINUTES, the API's "regain 2026-09-01" text is not predictive (`bugs_open/243`); the commit itself still carries no trailer, so `098` will not auto-credit it | `bugs_open/289` residual (5). `UpdateStateWithVersion` — the one path every state update takes — now logs when the `collected_data` it is about to write is abnormal: **WARN at 8 MiB, ERROR at 24 MiB**, naming the orchestration, agent type, step, total size, key count and **the single largest key**. 289 grew tool-auditor's state to 22 MB and killed 62 of 63 runs over 19 days with NOTHING noticing, because no threshold existed anywhere. Thresholds are set from a measured census (page-content-writer 1.8 MB max, build-dispatch-loop 14 MB, tool-auditor 22 MB avg) and a test PINS them, so a later edit cannot quietly move them under real traffic. Logs only — never fails a run, because the size is a symptom of another defect and killing the run destroys the evidence. Wiring proven by MUTATION: unwiring the single call site leaves every direct-call test passing and fails only the sqlmock-driven wiring test. LANDMINE: it is an INSTRUMENT, not a control — a run headed for 29 MB still dies, you merely find out. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-017 | Loop expansion suffixes ANY reference-shaped sibling-output config value (allow-list retired) | **LIVE + PROVEN v1.0.1307 + RATIFIED (owner ruling 2026-08-18, RFC_035 option 1 — the allow-list is retired as a contract)** — expanded plans read `result!: handler_result_0`; `field=result` resolver rows 0 (from ~455/day) with demand control, 11/11 loop completions carrying the reply, 0 spawn records; council r1 REJECTED on SCOPE and **owner-overridden on the merits** (cf. 124/BLD-019) — **Live ≠ approved, no Council-Reviewed trailer on that correlation** | `bugs_closed/287` (spawn_record). `prefixConfigStepReferences` rewrote sibling-output references only for an allow-list of config keys under a comment demanding every future key be listed — `complete_work_item`'s `result` was not, so `"result": "handler_result"` could never be suffixed and resolved only via the whole-tree search, whose deterministic winner is the SPAWN RECORD (`handler_spawned.result`, 176x/day). Now ANY top-level reference-shaped string value (bare identifier / dotted path; expressions and prose excluded by the shape gate) is rewritten via the existing `prefixDataReference`; `!`-strict keys ride automatically; the duplicated `dataRefKeys` loop is gone. Censused 22 sites / 7 agents, all read-references. ⚠ Suffixing alone zeroes NOTHING for a dotless mapping — the `!` flip (migrations 448 applied / 452_HOLD awaiting this roll) is the closer; and `condition` expressions are deliberately untouched. LANDMINE: a loop-substep literal that exactly names a sibling output_field is now a reference. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-018 | `config-key-audit --array-producer-conditions`: a condition dotting into a field its producer cannot carry | built + runnable 2026-08-19 (offline CLI + `scripts/audit-array-producer-conditions.sh`, nothing to roll); `Council-Submitted: aef24a7f` | `bugs_open/313`: `check_candidates` tested `candidate_pages.count > 0` against an `output_format: array` producer — a path no strategy resolves, so every run for four months silently took else_step and the agent's only LLM step never ran. Flags any string condition whose dotted left path lands on a field where EVERY producer is an array-format `query_database` (explicit or defaulted); walks nested substeps via `validation.WalkSteps`; skips numeric indexes (WFA-012), unknown producers, mixed producer sets. First live run: 191 agents, 145 conditionals, exactly 1 finding = the motivating case. Refuses over 0 conditionals so a broken decode cannot read as a pass. NOT cron-wired (deliberate, stated follow-up). | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-019 | opt-in `fail_on_non_numeric` on `conditional` — an unevaluable numeric comparison fails the step, never silently routes | built + tested 2026-08-19, **NOT live** (Go — rides the next chassis build; config half ships in migration 490 with `internal-linker.check_candidates` as the sole consumer, safe pre-roll since unknown keys are ignored — `bugs_open/101`); `Council-Submitted: aef24a7f`; lenient default FROZEN by test and both guards mutation-proven | `bugs_open/313`'s run-time closer. Numeric arms (`>`, `>=`, `<`, `<`) only: a side failing `ToFloat64` errors naming condition/field/value/type instead of `false, nil` → else_step. `==`/`!=`/truthy untouched — nil is a legitimate null probe there (LANDMINE: the flag will NOT catch a typo'd path in an equality condition; WFA-018 sees those statically). Read via `GetBoolFieldLoud` (WFA-010); exported `evaluateStringCondition` keeps its lenient 3-arg signature. Also the action's FIRST ActionInputSpec (both names; ConfigKeys complete per fleet census of all 145 live steps, nested included; no CheckConfig). Scope per WFA-009's precedent: owner 2026-07-29 §1, 2026-08-02 §2, RFC_022 with consumers enumerated. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-020 | Loop-filed work item keyed per-SITE detector (`config-key-audit --loop-sitewide-item-keys`, CronJob `loop-sitewide-item-key-check`) | **LIVE 2026-08-19, both halves** — daily 07:55 UTC, image pattern, in RELEASE_IMAGES (v1.0.1316 release retagged it unprompted); proven by FIRING (verbatim pre-493 fixture → 2 findings; suffix-stripped live export → the exact 6-step census; live fleet → 0); registry-pinning test guards the hardcoded action/keys | bugs_open/321's class check: a create_work_item nested in a loop carrying item_key_prefix without an HONOURED item_key_suffix_field silently drops iterations 2..N on idx_swi_dedup (tool-suggester measured 40→11, ~72%). Convicts ""/non-string suffixes (flagged); acquits top-level, no-prefix, loops-over-sites, sub_workflow-under-non-loop. WalkSteps single pass, parent from the qualified path. Wrapper reads refusal from EMPTY stdout. Blind spot (loop-invariant suffix) covered by the runtime Warn in CreateWorkItemAction (`shouldWarnLoopDedup`, Council-Submitted 43a7a60a, rides next roll). Config fix: migration 493 (all four steps + continue_on_error). | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-021 | `await_reconcile_enforce` — a workflow may make the advance decision adopt the `awaited_requests` TABLE when it disagrees with the in-memory map | **BUILT 2026-08-21, INERT UNTIL THE NEXT ROLL, and OFF everywhere** — no live workflow sets it and none should until a captured wedge justifies it; `Council-Submitted:` | Plan-level bool, default false = today's behaviour byte for byte. New authority on the shared park/response seam, so it ships opt-in with the UNSAFE SIDE OFF (owner ruling 2026-08-02 §2); RFC_022-narrowed ⇒ council-gate scope, not architecture — with the caveat that "zero live consumers" is TAUTOLOGICAL for a new field and must be stated as such. Detection does NOT depend on the flag; only the decision is gated. ⚠ two hazards before switching on: adopting a row means waiting out its timeout, after which the retry driver REPLAYS the request and real side effects re-run; and the table must never naively replace the map's CAS. ⚠ plan-level ⇒ OUTSIDE the WFA-013 optional-key budget; moving it to action config puts it inside and the cron parity test applies | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-022 | Commit-sha exposure detector (`config-key-audit --commit-sha-exposure`, CronJob `commit-sha-exposure-check`) — the STANDING form of migration 537's apply-time guard | **LIVE 2026-08-22, both halves** — mode hand-run on the live fleet (194 agents walked, the exact 8-handler intersection the SQL guard computes, 0 findings, exit 0; synthetic unexposed producer exits 1 naming it; 3 mutation proofs RUN) AND the CronJob (`v1.0.1324`, daily 06:45 UTC) proven in-cluster by a manual `--from=cronjob` run 09:41Z: pod Succeeded, **exit 0 read at the POD** (ImagePullBackOff reports here as a Job still RUNNING), doc_notes row written | `bugs_closed/334`'s residual: 537 wired bdl's `mark_complete` to `commit_sha?` = the handler's own reply or ABSENCE, and its guard proved the estate ready ONCE, at apply. The handler population is per-item and DYNAMIC, so a NEW commit-producing handler that does not expose `response.commit_sha` via its complete step's `result_mapping` silently never records `result.commit_sha` — no error, no row, because absence IS the contract. Same three sets as the guard, verbatim (30d producers = the handler's OWN orchestrations, NOT items that recorded a sha — that counts the contamination the wire removed; 7d dispatch handlers; result_mapping exposure), with ONE deliberate difference: WalkSteps, so a complete step nested in a sub-workflow is not a false alarm (`bugs_open/144`). ⚠ THREE structural vacuity refusals (empty producers / empty handlers / zero exposing agents fleet-wide) because this lane's own fixes silenced every observational control — a blind run exits 2, never a clean report. Acks file for reasoned exceptions, expected EMPTY (the right answer to a finding is a 519–540-style migration). | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-023 | `__step_errors` — a per-step failure record so "this step FAILED" stops being indistinguishable from "this step was SKIPPED" | **LIVE on v1.0.1337 (2026-08-25), both halves + config half (mig 588) applied; NOT yet proven in the discriminating case** (negative control discharged: first post-migration round approved with 0 unreadable; positive arm needs a real seat error and cannot be forced): reader `e521cde3e` (live on v1.0.1334 since that morning, failing closed), writer `dbd865ee8` — the writer was blocked all day because `coordinator.go` carried the `bugs_open/354` lane's uncommitted call to an UNTRACKED callee, and landed after an owner-approved sweep (`893a12d47`) of that lane's two files. ⚠ probe the WRITER's own literal (`step-error record capped at`, which read 0 pre-roll) — `__step_errors` returns 1 from the READER alone and reads as if it landed. Gate met on v1.0.1337 and **mig 588 APPLIED** (17/17 seats repointed; persist_submission + council_decide correctly keep complete_invalid). ⚠ its first apply FAILED and rolled back — the reviewed SKETCH's `UPDATE … FROM LATERAL` cannot reference the target table; a seat reviews a sketch, not executable SQL. | `routeToErrorStep` has always written `__step_error` as a SINGLE key OVERWRITTEN on every routed failure, so two failing steps keep only the last and no step can ask "did MY predecessor fail?". `__step_errors` accumulates a map keyed by failed step name, written ALONGSIDE (`__step_error` untouched, all 39 of its readers unaffected), **capped at 50 with a `__truncated` marker** because the path is fleet-wide and a loop makes a distinct step name per iteration. Exists so the council can separate an ABSTENTION (relevance filter skipped it — does not gate) from UNREADABLE (an opinion owed and LOST — downgrades an approval to REVISE); both leave the field absent, and without it the obvious `error_step` repoint converts a lost seat into a silent non-objection. ⚠ Sibling key rather than widening `__step_error` is MEASURED not preferred: **33** Go refs + **6** live agent configs read it. | workflow-authoring.md |
| WFA-024 | Prompt-template ↔ `input_fields` lint (`config-key-audit --template-input-fields`, `scripts/audit-template-input-fields.sh`) | built + runnable 2026-09-03 (offline CLI; NOT cron-wired — `--report` exists so scheduling is config-only) | `bugs_open/453` candidate 1: a template variable whose ROOT is outside `ExtractFields(input_fields)` renders as nothing (guarded/ranged) or `<no value>`, with no error and no verdict — 4 prior catches, all by fixtures written by luck. First run: 202 agents, 139 templates, 0 parse failures, 1 conviction — `page-content-writer` wanting `{{.research_result}}`, the same step 453 was filed about and a different root from the one fixed by hand a week earlier. LANDMINE: injected roots are PER ACTION, never a union (this lane's own first pass convicted both vision steps); a dotted `input_fields` entry supplies its LAST segment. Closes shape 2 of 3 and says so — shape 3 is undecidable from config. | workflow-authoring.md |

That layering is the point, not a side effect | The defect was one word: BLD-022's `*) continue;;` made membership of `RELEASE_IMAGES` the gate's OWN admission criterion, so a service omitted at birth was not *uncovered* but *out of scope* — and the gate printed "Release coverage OK" about it. **EIGHT services fell in; two of them (`optional-explicit-wires-check` 08-21, `commit-sha-exposure-check` 08-22) AFTER the owner ruling meant to close it, by sessions who had that ruling in front of them.** Now: our image + no `RELEASE_IMAGES` entry = violation, escapable only by an explicit `OWN_LINEAGE := <svc>:<retag target>` — opt-in with the UNSAFE SIDE DEFAULT OFF (owner 08-02 §2; not architecture-scope per RFC_022). `InvalidDeployEntries` closes BLD-022 §(iv)'s reverse direction (a deploy entry whose image left `RELEASE_IMAGES` → both GitHub runners `ImagePullBackOff` together, taking CI with them). The 25-line shell predicate is DELETED, not kept beside it. **OWN_LINEAGE was going to ship EMPTY and the first live run found a real entry**: `admin-dashboard` is genuinely own-lineage (frontend, working-tree build, unstamped) and genuinely released via `release-dashboard` — and nothing on disk said so, because the OLD gate could not see it. **PHASE 2 (08-22, `Council-Submitted: b0883c17`): the CLUSTER census** (`make release-census`) — the filesystem and the cluster are two enumerations and NEITHER CONTAINS THE OTHER (one service declared everywhere and running nowhere; two running with no overlay on disk). Reports BEHIND/AHEAD of the fleet tag, declared-but-not-running, running-but-not-declared. Predicates take a `[]Workload` not a k8s client, so they are table-testable with no cluster. **Its first live run asserted the INVERSE of the truth** — two hand-deployed services AHEAD of the fleet reported as "RUNNING AN OLD FLEET TAG"; a report that states the opposite is worse than one that stays quiet. Fixed by splitting the kind by DIRECTION (opposite remedies), ordering tags NUMERICALLY (this estate has crossed v1.0.999→v1.0.1000, where lexical says 999 is newer), and refusing to guess on an unorderable tag. Live: **29 of 45 workloads, 5 findings, zero false positives as of 2026-08-22**. ⚠ **HAND-RUN ONLY** — no CronJob/RBAC/doc_notes, scheduling is its own decision ("detection works; SCHEDULE and DISPATCH do not"); and it reads `containers[0]`, so a sidecar on one of our images would be missed. Two enumeration fixes: ANY DEPTH under `overlays/production` (`tools-api`'s real overlay has no region dir and the old glob could never see it) and `newName` beats `name` (latent — one placeholder overlay). ⚠ **`go run` COLLAPSES exit 2 into 1** [MEASURED], so 'could not run' vs 'release is wrong' is printed in WORDS; a scripted consumer must use the compiled binary. ⚠ A green run proves NOTHING (BLD-022 was corrected on exactly this) — the four mutation controls on a COPIED makefile are the evidence, incl. one that reproduces the original `237` bug state. **OWNER 2026-08-22: the staleness build is SKIPPED — CLOSED, not deferred** (*"we can skip the 18 August staleness build"*), and an ACCUMULATION BUDGET on the exemption list was taken instead: the release fails past **3** `OWN_LINEAGE` entries, and the gate NAMES the standing exemptions on every GREEN run so the fourth is noticed as it arrives rather than when a number is crossed (a threshold silent until it trips is one nobody watches). N is lower than WFA-013's 10 on purpose and is the owner's to change. ⚠ It cannot fire today — **one entry as of 2026-08-22** — and the reason that is acceptable here when BLD-023 refused `assert_live_capability()` on the same ground is that this needs NO CALLER: it runs on every `deploy-core` whether or not it fires. The staleness test was uncomputable anyway — `deploy-agents` seds `newTag` in place and nobody commits it, so the pin's history is fiction (**26 overlays dirty as of 2026-08-22**; `agent-chassis` committed at v1.0.1239 vs v1.0.1323 live). Do not re-file it | build-pipeline.md |

## Frozen measurement log (2026-07-13 → 2026-08-09)

**Historical. Do not extend this — it is the record of the retired practice, kept
because the way it failed is more useful than the numbers ever were.** Every
"previously N" below was a real measurement by a real thread following the
documented convention. The chain records concurrent arrivals, collisions caught
by the uniqueness check, and at least two figures taken from the wrong command.
It is the evidence for the retirement above.

**1,795 index table rows** — re-measured 2026-08-08 (~20:05 UTC) at commit time
by the **WII-012** lane (actionable-page identity honoured end-to-end,
`bugs_open/220` — row, entry and code in ONE commit, per the platform-seams
ruling), with the documented headline command. WII-012 confirmed unclaimed by
grep before landing; the command returned 1,792 immediately before my row and
**1,795 now** — the other two are **FTW-042 and SQAM-003, which landed in the
working tree from concurrent lanes between my edit and my commit** (their own
narrations sit below and each accounts for the other; neither had seen my row).
First draft of this paragraph said 1,793 and "nothing arrived concurrently" —
true when typed, false by commit time, corrected here rather than left to the
watcher. Previously **1,792** — re-measured 2026-08-08 (18:35 UTC) after adding
the missing `SCH-024` row, and the pair is clean: **1,792 rows, 1,792 unique
entry ids, 0 orphans either way.**

> **CORRECTED 2026-08-08, and the watcher found both halves of it.**
> `concept-register-drift-check` (DOC-074) has reported a headline mismatch on
> **three consecutive daily runs** (08-07, 08-08, and the manual run at 18:35):
> the header claimed **1,791** then **1,796** while the documented command
> returned **1,784** then **1,792**. Measured this minute, all four counts, so the
> next thread can see why they disagree:
>
> | command | count | what it counts |
> |---|---|---|
> | `grep -cE '^\| [A-Z]{2,4}-[0-9]{3} \|'` | **1,792** | **the headline. Only this one.** |
> | `grep -c '^\| [A-Z][A-Z]*-[0-9]'` | 1,799 | loose — over-counts by 7 |
> | unique `### ID` entry ids | 1,792 | the other half of the drift pair |
> | raw `### ` headings | 1,800 | includes non-concept headings |
>
> **The loose grep is quoted inside this very chain** (the RSH-007 line below
> names it), which is how its number keeps arriving in the headline. It has now
> done so twice in four days, by two different careful sessions. The lesson is not
> "be careful": it is that **a hand-maintained count sitting beside three other
> counts of the same file will drift, and no convention fixes that.**
>
> The second half is worse and is the reason the watcher exists: **`SCH-024` had
> an entry and no index row** — filed 2026-08-08 by the `bugs_open/205` lane
> (`b6e70cd70`), four days after 34 such rows were backfilled. Row added here in
> the same pass. The class recurs; it is not a one-off that was cleared.

Re-grepped 2026-09-03 (~12:4x UTC) after **TP-009** (fence value-assertion grading — `summariseCriteriaValueAssertions` plus the `liveness_only` verdict scope, `bugs_open/449`; row, entry and code in ONE commit per the platform-seams ruling, and TP-009 confirmed unclaimed before landing — `grep -c TP-009` returned 0 in the index and 0 in `tool-pipeline.md` **at HEAD**, which is the check that matters on a shared tree, since my own entry was already in the working tree when I looked), with the documented headline command `grep -cE '^\| [A-Z]{2,4}-[0-9]{3} \|'`: **2,070 rows, 2,069 unique row ids, 2,069 unique `### ID` entry ids** — so the triple is NOT clean and **the discrepancy is a DUPLICATE ROW, not an orphan entry, which is a shape this chain had not recorded before: `WII-035` appears TWICE**, at lines 420 and 421, as a near-identical pair reading like a same-id rewrite where the superseded row was never removed. **It is not mine and it predates this edit** — `git show HEAD:` returns 2 occurrences, so it arrived before I touched the file — and I have not deleted either one: choosing which of two descriptions of another lane's mechanism is the current one is exactly the guess this practice refuses. Flagging so `WII-035`'s owning lane drops the stale row. ⚠ **Method note:** the uniqueness check here must compare rows against *unique* row ids, not rows against entry ids — I nearly read 2,070-vs-2,069 as a missing entry, which would have sent me hunting for an entry that exists. A duplicate row and a missing entry produce the same one-off arithmetic and want opposite fixes. TP-009 added ONE and the count was 2,069 immediately before, which is **89 above the 1,981 recorded next** — ten days of concurrent lanes, and again the argument for re-grepping rather than carrying the number. Verified present as BOTH row and entry in the same pass. > not the working tree, so it cannot see an entry until it is committed and
> **cannot be used to check your own uncommitted addition** — it will report the
> pre-change state as clean and that pass says nothing about your edit. Bump this
> headline in the same commit as the entry and the row, then re-run it after
> committing to confirm.

mid-edit, from another session's row, which is the same
